=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
You are given an integer array nums and two integers, k and m.
Return the maximum sum of k non-overlapping subarrays of nums, where each subarray has a length of at least m.
 
Example 1:

Input: nums = [1,2,-1,3,3,4], k = 2, m = 2
Output: 13
Explanation:
The optimal choice is:

Subarray nums[3..5] with sum 3 + 3 + 4 = 10 (length is 3 >= m).
Subarray nums[0..1] with sum 1 + 2 = 3 (length is 2 >= m).

The total sum is 10 + 3 = 13.

Example 2:

Input: nums = [-10,3,-1,-2], k = 4, m = 1
Output: -10
Explanation:
The optimal choice is choosing each element as a subarray. The output is (-10) + 3 + (-1) + (-2) = -10.

 
Constraints:

1 <= nums.length <= 2000
-10^4 <= nums[i] <= 10^4
1 <= k <= floor(nums.length / m)
1 <= m <= 3

Use the following starter code to write the final solution.
```python
class Solution:
    def maxSum(self, nums: List[int], k: int, m: int) -> int:
        
```

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

Now, is this DP complete? Let's reason.

We process each element with choices. But one subtlety: When an open subarray reaches length m, we cap l=m. If actual length > m, l=m. We allow "close before x" at any later position, meaning the subarray ended at previous position. That's valid. But can we "close before x and start x" from l=m; old ended previous position, new starts current. This allows adjacent subarrays. Good.
Can we "include x and close" from l=m; old includes current and ends current. Then next position can start new from l=0. Good.
Can we "include x and continue" from l=m; old includes current and remains open. Good.

But what about closing an open subarray at the same time as starting a new one at the same position? We have "close before x and start x" (old ends previous, new starts current) and "include x and close" (old ends current, no new). What about old ends current and new starts current? Cannot because overlapping at current; invalid. What about old ends previous and new starts previous? impossible. So okay.

Now, does DP allow a gap between subarrays? Yes, from l=0 skip elements; from l=m close before x and skip x (gap after old), then l=0 skip more. If old ends at i-1, skip i, start later. Good.

But there is a potential issue with "close before x and skip x" from l=m: At step i, we close old at previous position and skip current. But what if old was already closed at previous step? State l=0 would represent no open; if old closed at previous position, j would have been incremented at previous step. Our DP increments j when closing. So okay.

Let's formalize transitions with exact conditions.

Pseudo:

class Solution:
    def maxSum(self, nums: List[int], k: int, m: int) -> int:
        NEG = -10**18
        dp = [[NEG]*(m+1) for _ in range(k+1)]
        dp[0][0] = 0
        for x in nums:
            ndp = [[NEG]*(m+1) for _ in range(k+1)]
            for j in range(k+1):
                for l in range(m+1):
                    cur = dp[j][l]
                    if cur == NEG: continue
                    if l == 0:
                        # skip x
                        if cur > ndp[j][0]: ndp[j][0] = cur
                        # start new subarray at x
                        if cur + x > ndp[j][1]: ndp[j][1] = cur + x
                    else:
                        # continue current subarray through x
                        nl = m if l + 1 >= m else l + 1
                        # Actually if l==m, l+1 >=m, nl=m. If l<m, nl=l+1. Since l<=m. min(m,l+1)
                        if cur + x > ndp[j][nl]: ndp[j][nl] = cur + x
                        # close current subarray at x (include x)
                        if l == m or l + 1 >= m:  # l >= m-1
                            if j+1 <= k:
                                if cur + x > ndp[j+1][0]: ndp[j+1][0] = cur + x
                        # close current subarray before x (does not include x)
                        if l == m:
                            if j+1 <= k:
                                if cur > ndp[j+1][0]: ndp[j+1][0] = cur
                                # close before x and start new at x
                                if cur + x > ndp[j+1][1]: ndp[j+1][1] = cur + x
            dp = ndp
        ans = dp[k][0]
        if k-1 >= 0:
            ans = max(ans, dp[k-1][m])
        return ans

Need check if "close current subarray at x" from l==m and "close before x and start new at x" from l==m both can lead to same j+1 and l=0/1. Good.

But wait, from l==m, "close current subarray at x" and "continue current subarray through x" both add x. If we close at x, j increments; if continue, j same. Good.

Now, is there a transition from l==m to l=0 by "close before x and skip x" without adding x; this closes old at previous position. But what if old subarray ended at previous position and we skip current, then at next position l=0. Good. But what if we want to close old at previous position and also not skip current but start new at current? We have close before x and start x. Good.

Now, is there a transition from l==m to l=0 by "close current subarray at x" and then skip x? No, close at x includes x; after processing x, state l=0. It doesn't skip x. Good.

Pseudo:

for x in nums:
    ndp = [[NEG]*(m+1) for _ in range(k+1)]
    for j in range(k+1):
        for l in range(m+1):
            cur = dp[j][l]
            if cur == NEG: continue
            if l == 0:
                # skip
                if cur > ndp[j][0]: ndp[j][0] = cur
                # start
                if j < k:
                    val = cur + x
                    if val > ndp[j][1]: ndp[j][1] = val
            else:
                # continue
                if j < k:  # open will be counted, so cannot have j==k
                    nl = m if l + 1 >= m else l + 1
                    val = cur + x
                    if val > ndp[j][nl]: ndp[j][nl] = val
                # close at x (include x)
                if j + 1 <= k and (l == m or l + 1 >= m):
                    val = cur + x
                    if val > ndp[j+1][0]: ndp[j+1][0] = val
                # close before x and skip
                if j + 1 <= k and l == m:
                    if cur > ndp[j+1][0]: ndp[j+1][0] = cur
                # close before x and start new at x
                if j + 1 < k and l == m:  # need room for new open
                    val = cur + x
                    if val > ndp[j+1][1]: ndp[j+1][1] = val

Before final, let's thoroughly prove DP invariant to ensure no hidden flaw.

Invariant: After processing first i elements, `dp[j][l]` is max sum of a selection from these i elements such that:
- There are j completed subarrays (closed before or at i? Actually completed subarrays are those that have been closed and counted; their elements are all within first i elements).
- If l=0, no subarray is currently open.
- If l>0, there is one open subarray that includes the i-th element (the last processed element) and has current length state l (capped at m). Its sum is included in dp value.
- All selected subarrays are non-overlapping and completed ones have length >=m; the open one may be incomplete if l<m or valid if l=m.
- No other open subarrays.
Then transitions process next element x = nums[i] (0-index):
From l=0:
- skip x: no open, j same.
- start x: open length1, j same, add x. Valid only if j<k because open will count eventually.
From l>0:
- continue x: open length increases, add x. Valid only if j<k.
- close at x: if after adding x length reaches m (l+1>=m or l==m), close and count j+1, no open. Add x.
- close before x and skip: if l==m (open already valid), close at previous position, count j+1, x skipped.
- close before x and start x: if l==m, close old at previous position, count j+1, start new at x, add x. Need j+1<k because new open will count later.
At end, a valid final selection either has no open and j=k, or has one open valid (l=m) and j=k-1. This covers all.

Potential issue: The transition "close before x and skip" closes open at previous position. But if l==m, the open subarray includes previous element. If we close it before processing x, then after processing x (skipped), the open subarray is closed and counted. This means the subarray's last element is i-1. Good. But in invariant, after processing first i elements, completed subarrays are closed; if we close at "before x", the subarray ended at i-1, which is within first i elements. Good.

Potential issue: The transition "close before x and start x" closes old at i-1 and starts new at i. After processing i, there is an open subarray including i. Good.

Potential issue: The transition "close at x" from l>0 closes open including x. After processing i, no open. Good.

Potential issue: If l>0 and l<m, we cannot close before x because open length <m. We only allow close at x if l+1>=m. Good.

Potential issue: If l==m, we can close before x or at x. Good.

=== ANSWER ===
is final code. Need output only code. Ensure code correct.

Let's write final code:

```python
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def maxSum(self, nums: List[int], k: int, m: int) -> int:
        NEG = -10**18
        dp = [[NEG] * (m + 1) for _ in range(k + 1)]
        dp[0][0] = 0

        for x in nums:
            ndp = [[NEG] * (m + 1) for _ in range(k + 1)]
            for j in range(k + 1):
                for l in range(m + 1):
                    cur = dp[j][l]
                    if cur == NEG:
                        continue

                    if l == 0:
                        # skip x
                        if cur > ndp[j][0]:
                            ndp[j][0] = cur
                        # start a new subarray at x
                        if j < k:
                            val = cur + x
                            if val > ndp[j][1]:
                                ndp[j][1] = val
                    else:
                        # extend the current subarray through x
                        if j < k:
                            nl = m if l + 1 >= m else l + 1
                            val = cur + x
                            if val > ndp[j][nl]:
                                ndp[j][nl] = val

                        # close the current subarray at x
                        if j + 1 <= k and l + 1 >= m:
                            val = cur + x
                            if val > ndp[j + 1][0]:
                                ndp[j + 1][0] = val

                        # close the current subarray before x, then skip x
                        if j + 1 <= k and l == m:
                            if cur > ndp[j + 1][0]:
                                ndp[j + 1][0] = cur

                        # close the current subarray before x, then start a new one at x
                        if j + 1 < k and l == m:
                            val = cur + x
                            if val > ndp[j + 1][1]:
                                ndp[j + 1][1] = val
            dp = ndp

        ans = dp[k][0]
        if k >= 1:
            ans = max(ans, dp[k - 1][m])
        return ans
```

Need double-check condition for close at x: `l + 1 >= m`. If l=m and m=1, l+1=2>=1. If l=0? We are in l>0 branch. Good. If l=m-1, valid. If l<m-1, invalid. Good.

But wait, if l=m and m=3, l+1=4>=3. Good. If l=2, 3>=3. Good.

Now, let's think if `j + 1 < k` for close-before-start is correct. Suppose k=1, j=0, l=m, close before start would create open second subarray, invalid. Condition false. Good.
Suppose k=2, j=0, l=m, close before start creates j=1, l=1; total eventual 2. Condition 1<2 true. Good.
Suppose k=2, j=1, l=m, close before start creates j=2, l=1; total eventual 3 invalid. Condition 2<2 false. Good.

Now, what about "continue" from l>0 when j<k. If j=k-1, l=m, continue valid. If j=k-1, l<m, continue valid. Good.
